# Decision Log

A running record of decisions made while building this system, one entry per
build-order step (see section 19 of the technical specification). Append only.

---

This log was split from a single `docs/decision-log.md` file into one file per
major build-order step, so a session can read only the range it needs instead of
the whole history. No entry was reworded — this was a pure file split. Sub-steps
live in the same file as their parent step.

Order is preserved as written (append-only): within Step 10, entry **10.7**
appears before **10.6**, matching the order the work happened.

**Adding new entries:** append to the `NN.md` matching the step number (sub-steps
go in their parent's file); start a new `NN.md` for a new major step and add it to
the index below. Do not write to the old `docs/decision-log.md` stub.

| [10-frontend.md](10-frontend.md) | 10.6, 10.8, 10.9, 10.10, 10.11, 10.12, 10.13, 10.14 | Dashboard frontend scaffolding and bot list; bot detail view; liquidate action; global kill switch control; create-bot form; cross-bot alert feed; manual-adjustment form; **10.14** — derived position figures and read-only configuration on the bot detail view: the DCA take-profit PRICE (average entry x (1 + tp%)), unrealized mark-to-market profit for both strategies, and a read-only Configuration block per strategy. UI-only and purely additive. `src/shared/money.ts` becomes the **second file the dashboard imports across the seam** after `alert-types.ts`, earning it on the same terms (dependency-free by contract, verified by building) and for the same reason — a re-derived decimal parser would not throw, it would quietly disagree by a rounding step on the intervention screen. Importing `dca.ts` itself to call the bot's own `takeProfitPrice` was **tried and rejected** for a concrete reason: the dashboard's `noUnusedLocals` is stricter than the Worker's and fails on a dead `mulInt` import in `dca.ts`, so `applyPercent` is a documented one-line mirror instead — pinned by an 18-test parity suite that runs BOTH implementations over the same awkward inputs. Every convention taken from backend source rather than taste: **ceil** (the gain must be at least what was configured), **one** rounding step not two, and `mul(price, qty, "half-even")` — the same call and mode the books use for cost. The figure is **gross** and labelled so, because `cost` is the bare notional and `feeAmount` never enters the position. The percentage is of **cost basis**, not price-vs-average-entry: exact against money actually spent, and the only form grid can use since a ladder stores no average entry. Mutation testing caught **two** faults in sequence — the value test's product was exact so every mode agreed on it (fixed with two half-way tie cases that break in opposite directions), and then the harness itself, where `perl -0p` without `/g` had been mutating a docblock instead of the code line; the re-run verifies the mutation landed before trusting the result. 9 mutants all caught; 1416 → **1434 pass**. **Two corrections landed after the fact, both from checking live rather than assuming:** the session's verification plan claimed both bots were halted and unrealized PnL would read static — false, they had been resumed ~12.6h earlier (D1: `status = running`, `bot.resumed` 08-01 01:39 UTC), because the session **read step 18's append-only entry as current state**; the general rule now recorded is that anything which can change after an entry is written must be read from the system, never quoted from this log. And a **new open question 4**: `halt_manual`/`halt_order_rejected` alerts have **no lifecycle at all** — `#halt` writes them with an unconditional `#alert`, not step 18's `raiseStandingAlert`, and `resume` never resolves them, so a running, recovered bot still shows stale unresolved criticals in the alert feed and detail view. Same shape as step 16's bug 3, one layer out |

| [14.md](14.md) | 14, 14.1–14.10 | Price feed design (execution wiring, part 2 of 2) — the outbound-WebSocket-does-not-hibernate correction (spec §4.6 deviation), shared `PriceFeed` DO keyed by (exchange, pair), `subscribeToPriceFeed`/`WebSocketHandle` deletion + `getCandles` addition, closed-1m-candle→Price, Gemini-first, and single-alarm reconnect/backoff/backfill with an alert-only blind-feed policy; the Gemini-WS reachability probe (PASS: `candles_1m_updates`, no closed flag, OHLCV as JSON numbers); **14.1 Session A** — the interface reshape + `getCandles`/`parseCandles`/`parseKlines` build; **14.2 Session B** — the pure `GeminiPriceFeedCodec` (`socketUrl`/`subscribeMessage`/`parseMessage`, `FeedEvent` 4-way, reusing `parseCandles`) tested against real captured payloads; the deliberate multi-boundary probe (Q1 **CURRENT-ONLY** 6/6, Q2 **~5s heartbeat**); and **14.3 Session C1** — the `PriceFeed` Durable Object (CURRENT-ONLY stream engine, outbound socket lifecycle, single multiplexed alarm, backoff, **WS-batch gap-backfill deviating from decision 5**, blind-feed + 30-min escalation), 15 tests; **14.4 Session C2** — the durable SQLite subscriber registry + `allSettled` fan-out (`subscribe` carries config, `idFromName` cross-DO calls, first-open/last-close gating, `stopFeed` watermark reset, no-auto-prune on fanout failure), 20 tests total; **14.5 Session D** — wiring `BotInstance` status transitions to the feed (subscribe fail-closed in `start`/`resume`, unsubscribe best-effort in `#halt`/`close`, DCA auto-restart stays subscribed), 7 wiring tests. **The price-feed arc (14.1–14.5) is complete**; a live end-to-end sandbox run remains a deployed, manual step; **14.6** — the temporary `GET /api/debug/ws-check` **Tier 0** edge→Gemini WebSocket reachability diagnostic: confirmed **PASS** live (2026-07-30, `candles_1m_updates` received) and removed the same day, but it caught a real feed-transport bug (`wss://` passed to `fetch`) whose fix — the shared `httpUrlForWebSocket` scheme translation + handshake tests in `price-feed.ts` — stays permanently; **14.7** — the temporary `GET /api/debug/feed-check` **Tier 1** diagnostic verifying the REAL `PriceFeed` DO live (throwaway `debug-check` subscriber, watermark-advance as fan-out evidence via a temporary read-only `debugSnapshot()`, `price_feed_fanout_failed` in D1 as per-subscriber attribution, 120s bounded wait with an `inconclusive` verdict for a quiet market, unsubscribe in a `finally`), 14 tests: confirmed **PASS** live (2026-07-30, watermark 1785463079999→1785463139999 — exactly one 60s candle — with D1's `price_feed_fanout_failed` corroborating the fan-out to `debug-check`) and fully removed the same day, route AND read path, leaving the DO exactly as it was. **The feed itself is now proven live**; only feed→bot→order and the ~15-minute eviction ceiling remain unobserved; **14.8** — that feed→bot→order run **attempted live (2026-07-31)**: the DCA base order reached Gemini and was refused with `MissingAccounts`, the bot halting correctly. Gemini's docs confirm the field is **`account` (singular, a top-level string, the account nickname), required for MASTER keys only** and refused on account-level keys (`AccountsOnGroupOnlyApi`) — so it is added once in `GeminiClient.#request` for every signed endpoint (order/new, order/status, order/cancel, orders, balances — all five had the same gap, balances marking it required), derived from the key's `master-`/`account-` prefix via the pure `resolveAccountField`, configured by `GEMINI_ACCOUNT_NAME`, and failing closed at both the resolver (one `not_attached` halt) and the client (non-retryable, nothing sent). Tests use hand-written-to-spec + `openssl` payload vectors and whole-payload `toEqual` (the old `toMatchObject` assertion stayed green through the live failure), mutation-checked against three near-miss implementations. **Verified against docs and tests, NOT yet against Gemini**; the bot stays halted; **14.9** — two pre-existing `verify-gemini.ts` faults found re-running it, neither related to 14.8: Tier 0's 404 is the **sandbox carrying no ticker data for real pairs** (`'btcusd' does not have available data yet`, plain text; `/v1/pricefeed` lists only EFILFIL/GUSDUSD) and NOT a stale path — `/v1/pubticker/{symbol}` is current and works on production — so Tier 0 now uses symbol-details + `/v2/candles` (sandbox-served, real BTCUSD prices) and reports the ticker 404 as a known venue gap; **consequence: the human-liquidation path reads `getCurrentPrice`, so liquidation cannot be exercised end to end on Gemini testnet** (degrades to `liquidation_no_price` by design). And Tier 1's "not set" was the script's own documented `VAR=... esbuild && node` invocation scoping the variables to `esbuild`, not a reading bug — now documented as two commands with the environment on `node`, with a skip message naming the trap; **14.10** — the first live signed call got past `MissingAccounts` to `InvalidAccountName` ("Expected a JSON **array** with valid accounts, instead got: Primary"), which **looks like** it contradicts 14.8 but does not: a machine-extracted re-check of Gemini's whole reference (305 pages from the sitemap) finds **65 endpoints typing `account` as `string`, zero typing it `array`, zero fields named `accounts`, zero array request examples**. The real cause is that a Gemini account has **two names** — display `name` `"Primary"` vs API nickname `account` `"primary"`, with a documented derivation — and `GEMINI_ACCOUNT_NAME` held the display name; the plural/array wording names the error code and Gemini's internal list validation, not the wire type (cf. `MoreThanOneAccount`). **Payload shape unchanged.** Added a local pre-send nickname check (`[a-z0-9-]+`, refusing with a derived suggestion and deliberately NOT auto-normalising), `listMasterGroupAccounts()` + a `groupLevel` spec flag so `/v1/account/list` correctly sends no `account`, and a Tier 1 pre-step printing `name` beside `account`. Tests assert against **Gemini's own published name→account pairs**; six mutants all caught, including "send an array" and "rename to accounts". Also: the 14.9 sandbox ticker gap **closed on its own** mid-session (404 → live quotes), confirming that diagnosis |
| [19.md](19.md) | 19 | **Observing fills on resting orders, part 1 of 3.** Root cause behind both `bot-44400a` and the step-17/18 three-fills incident: **nothing observes a fill on an order that RESTS.** `onFill` has four callers, all order-placement sites, each only draining `result.fills` from the placement RESPONSE; there is no user-trade socket and no status poll. For DCA that is a **liveness** failure, not a latency one — `decide` returns early on `position.quantity <= ZERO` before it reaches the stop-loss or take-profit branches, so a bot whose base order rests sits in `hold` forever while genuinely holding coin, until reconciliation halts it. **One premise checked and corrected before designing:** "reconciliation only notices hours later" is FALSE — `balance_snapshots` shows unbroken 5-minute passes across the incident (`08:20:47` clean, `08:25:47` finding the drift); the order rested ~4h55m because the price never reached it. So this is not about detection latency; §9 stays a competent backstop that halts, alerts and **never auto-corrects**, and this path is what keeps a RUNNING bot's books current so the backstop has less to find. Built: `#pollOpenOrders` reading each `openOrderIds` entry via `getOrderStatus` at **`routine`** priority (keeping books current must not draw on §5.4's risk-exit reserve), folding unseen executions through the ordinary live path — `applyFill`, `applyEntry`/`planFill`, `#mirrorOrderUpdate`, `#mirrorTrade` reused verbatim, so **no new arithmetic exists** and a poll-recorded position cannot disagree with a pushed one; idempotency is step 18's `fillId` identity check, unchanged. **`placeReplacement` is derived, not hardcoded** — step 18 passes `false` (a repair must not trade from a halted bot), the instinct here is `true` (a resting grid buy that fills SHOULD place its paired sell — the grid working, not a back-door resume), and the actual rule is `fresh.status === "running"`, since a poll on a halted bot must still place nothing; hardcoding `true` passes every running-case test and silently breaks the halted one, which a mutant confirms. **Two exchanges, two capabilities:** Gemini's `getOrderStatus` always sent `include_trades` so real `tid`s are available and a Gemini bot fully repairs, but **Binance's `GET /api/v3/order` carries no fills array at all** — so "more filled than recorded, no id behind it" is real, and is alerted (`unattributable_fill`) and SKIPPED rather than given a synthesised id, for the reason `#recordCancellation` already documents. `#foldTerminalState` closes orders the exchange ended (clearing the ladder slot for a grid, since the ladder owns `openOrderIds`) **gated on the fill quantities agreeing** — an order that ended with more filled than recorded is left OPEN, because terminal states have empty successor lists and `applyFill` throws `fill_after_terminal`, making the understatement **permanent**; that is exactly how `v1-bot-44400a-0`'s 0.00031777 BTC became unattributable when the halt sweep closed it. `bot.open_orders_checked` audited **only when something moved** (at 30s a row per pass would measure uptime, not events). 13 tests, 1440 → **1453**, four mutants caught — and the fold-gate mutant is recorded because **it survived the first test written for it**: that test set up the bot-44400a shape faithfully and asserted the order stayed open, which was true, but reached it through the `unattributable_fill` `continue` and never executed the gate; the replacement drives a venue that DID report per-fill detail whose fill overfills the order. **Deliberately not done:** not scheduled (no alarm, no production caller — step 20), `applyMissedFills` and the halt-sweep interaction untouched at the operator's explicit direction, and **this does not recover bot-44400a's BTC**. Open questions: the 0.00031777 BTC has no in-system home (`manual_adjustments` is account+asset scoped, no `bot_instance_id`/price/fill id, so it can make capital accounting truthful but cannot reconstruct a DCA position — operator's call, not a code change); **alert lifecycle is a precondition for step 20** (`raiseStandingAlert` is module-private to `reconcile.ts`, so until it is shared an `unattributable_fill` would write ~2,880 rows/bot/day, step 18's problem in a new place); and 30s is a decision, not a measurement |
| [18.md](18.md) | 18, 18.1 | **The order-state-drift correction path, and standing alerts.** Two consequences of 17 now that reconciliation can see the exchange. **`applyMissedFills`** — `POST /api/bots/:id/apply-missed-fills` → `BotInstance.applyMissedFills(actor)`, a thin wrapper like start/resume/liquidate, human-initiated because §9 halts on order-state drift and **deliberately never auto-corrects** it. Two existing constraints shaped it more than convenience: (1) **fill ids are never synthesised** — `#recordCancellation`'s docblock already established that `applyFill` dedupes on `fillId` so a made-up id makes the real fill "either double-count or be silently swallowed", so the repair uses Gemini's own `tid`s read via `getOrderStatus` (which always sent `include_trades`), which also makes it **idempotent for free** via the same identity check the live path uses, not a flag; (2) **a grid fill normally PLACES the paired sell**, and `#halt` never clears the ladder (confirmed — `#cancelOpenOrders` only trims `openOrderIds`, and every cancellation failed on 07-31), so running it unmodified would put live orders on the exchange from a HALTED bot — resuming trading by the back door. `placeReplacement: false` suppresses only that; verified clean rather than a fudge because `planFill` clears the filled level itself and returns the replacement as a separate intent, so **no phantom order** is left (asserted, since a phantom would make the next pass raise fresh drift). Everything else reused verbatim — `applyFill`, `applyEntry`/`planFill`, `#mirrorOrderUpdate`, `#mirrorTrade` — so **no new arithmetic exists** and a repaired position cannot disagree with a normally-recorded one. Refuses on a running bot, never changes status, reports unreadable orders rather than assuming, and distinguishes "no `trades` reported" from "no executions". **Standing alerts** — the same 3 findings had written **186 identical unresolved criticals in 4 hours** (~864/day). Only half unintended: §10's KV cooldown throttles the outbound *ping* correctly and `notifications/cooldown.ts` is explicit that recording and notifying are separate concerns; the unthrottled part was the D1 row. Unconditional insert is right for discrete events, wrong for a condition re-detected on a schedule and never auto-corrected, because the row count then measures duration rather than what is wrong and "how many unresolved criticals" stops meaning anything. `raiseStandingAlert` writes one row per open incident keyed `(alert_type, bot_instance_id)` — the cooldown's own key, message deliberately excluded so a drifting run id is still the same incident. **Nothing historical is lost**: every run's full findings list is already in `audit_log.details_json`, so `alerts` becomes what `resolved` always implied. `resolveClearedAlerts` closes them, **gated on the pass having actually read something** — a skipped-read run found nothing because it saw nothing, and resolving on that would clear a live incident on the strength of an outage (§5.6 applied to the alert lifecycle). 15 tests, 4 mutants caught. **Bots deliberately NOT resumed and the correction NOT yet run** — invoking it is the operator's action; note the grid's paired sell will not be placed by the repair nor on resume (`#placeInitialLadder` runs once), so `bot-328qgw` returns one rung short, truthfully represented. *(Since verified live, 2026-08-01: the operator did both — missed fills applied 01:29 UTC, both bots resumed 01:39 UTC, drift criticals now resolved; both read `status = running` in D1. **This row records a 2026-07-31 decision, not current state** — see 10.14 open question 1 for how treating it as current state went wrong.)* **18.1** — the dashboard control for that endpoint, built once the raw API had been used in anger (closing 18's open question 1): it renders **only while the bot has an UNRESOLVED order-state-drift alert** (five types, built tier×kind; the `severe_` pair unreachable under `TIER_CEILING` but listed so a future change cannot silently hide the button), reusing the alerts already on `GET /api/bots/:id`. `cancel_fill_discrepancy`/`cancel_failed` deliberately **excluded** — the repair reads `openOrderIds` only, so it provably cannot reach a cancelled order (18's own open question 2), and a button that returns an empty `applied` is worse than no button. Refuses a non-halted bot in **two layers** (the button is replaced by copy, not shown-and-disabled; the 409 branch remains for the load-to-click race). The grid dialog states three separate facts — the paired sell is not placed, the rung stays empty and **resume will not re-place it** (`#placeInitialLadder` runs once), and this is deliberate because placing a live order from a correction path on a halted bot resumes trading by the back door — plus the honest upside that no **phantom** order is left. Outcomes are the real `MissedFillsResult` in four shapes, `applied` as a table down to the exchange's own fill ids, `skipped` **verbatim** (the backend's "detail not reported" ≠ "nothing filled" distinction lives in its prose and is not re-derived by string-matching), and a dropped request as "outcome unknown" **plus** the useful fact that retrying is safe because the repair dedupes on fill id. The cross-bot feed's open drift rows link straight to `#apply-missed-fills` and scroll to it — they never open the dialog, since a confirmation a link can open is not a confirmation. **Six endpoint tests** added (the DO had 15, the endpoint had none), each asserting a claim the UI makes to an operator; 1403 → **1409**. **Addendum (same day):** 18.1's open question 2 — the `reconciliation_{tier}_{kind}` string built independently on both sides — **closed rather than carried**, because unlike every other hand-mirrored contract here (a renamed API field surfaces as a runtime `undefined`) this one fails **silently**: the repair button just stops appearing on a bot that has drift, indistinguishable on screen from having none. It was worse than recorded — **four** copies, not two (both raise sites, `resolveClearedAlerts`'s two `startsWith` checks, and the dashboard). `src/shared/alert-types.ts` is now the only spelling of the format, and **the one file the dashboard imports from the Worker's source tree** — dependency-free by contract so both toolchains compile it despite the deliberate TS 5.7/7 split; `vite dev` serving it over `/@fs/` was checked with a real request, not assumed. Since it cannot import `findings.ts`, the tie runs the other way as two type assertions that do real work (`ORDER_STATE_DRIFT_FINDING_KINDS`, `ALERTING_DRIFT_TIERS`), so renaming a `FindingKind` fails the typecheck instead of emptying a set. **Seven tests with derived lists, never hand-maintained** (a second copied list would reproduce the bug): six in `shared/alert-types.test.ts` importing the dashboard's own predicate, sweeping every kind from `TIER_FLOOR`'s keys in both directions, plus one in `reconcile.test.ts` driving real detection → real `raiseStandingAlert` → real `alertView` → the dashboard gate. Four mutants caught, including a typecheck failure before any test runs; 1409 → **1416** |
| [17.md](17.md) | 17 | **Two Gemini parsing bugs, uncovered by deploying step 16's dispatch fix** — both were UNREACHABLE while reconciliation talked to Binance, so the fix did not create them, it was the first code ever to exercise these parsers against a real Gemini response. Deploying 16 halted both bots within one pass; that was progress, not regression. **Bug 1** — `asRecord` refused arrays with `expected an object, got ${typeof value}`, and `typeof []` is `"object"`, producing the useless **`expected an object, got object`**. The real shape could not be looked up (no raw body retained, docs JS-rendered, endpoint signed) so the honest `describeShape` message was deployed FIRST as its own step purely to learn it, and the next scheduled run reported it in its own alert text: **a one-element array** wrapping the documented order-status object, keys `[avg_execution_price, client_order_id, exchange, executed_amount, id, is_cancelled, is_hidden, is_live, options, order_id, original_amount, price, remaining_amount, side, symbol, timestamp, timestampms, trades, type, was_forced]`. `unwrapSingleOrder` accepts BOTH shapes (same 14.10 lesson: establish the wire shape from a real response, not the prose) and deliberately does NOT do `body[0]` — empty and multi-element arrays are refused with specific messages, since silently reporting "no such order" is exactly what §9 exists to catch. **Bug 2** — Gemini returned USD `"99829.54180779832"` (11 dp) and `fromDecimalString`'s "rounding must be explicit" made the whole account unreadable. Strictness is right for values we SEND, wrong for values an exchange REPORTS; added `fromDecimalStringRounded` reusing the module's existing `Rounding` union (not a parallel vocabulary), `"exact"` still throws, balances use **`"floor"`** because rounding up would overstate funds and let the ledger believe in money that is not there. Own test caught an ordering bug: `"exact"` wrongly threw on `"1.5000000000000"`, which loses nothing. All tests use the REAL captured values; 3 mutants caught. **Audit answer: these are NOT provably the only two** — every `requireMoney` in `parse.ts` reads a reported value, with `avg_execution_price` (a computed average) the most likely to fire next; deliberately left unchanged for want of a real payload to test against. **Live result at 20:40Z: `skipped: 0` on both accounts**, `gemini-main` showing 6 real Gemini assets vs `main`'s 445 Binance ones — and reconciliation immediately found **3 of the 5 resting orders are FILLED on Gemini** while the bots still believed them `pending`, retiring the "quiet market" theory the whole investigation opened with. Also: `main` (Binance) had **never been reconciled at all** before step 16 (no bot, no ledger row); it is now, cleanly — at ~128k snapshot rows/day, giving `balance_snapshots` retention a real number |
| [16.md](16.md) | 16 | **Two production bugs found live, plus one UI-correctness reversal.** Found while answering a smaller question (is the hanging Gemini sandbox UI our fault?) — `verify-gemini.ts` Tier 0 PASS proved Gemini healthy, and the D1 comparison that followed found `balance_snapshots` holding **445 assets under `gemini-main`** including `1MBABYDOGE`/`1000CHEEMS`/`0G`, none of which exist in Gemini's 392-symbol sandbox roster (confirmed by unsigned public call). **Bug 1** — `reconciliation.ts` called `resolveDefaultExchange` (Binance) once above the account loop and used it for every account regardless of registered exchange, while `BotInstance` had dispatched correctly since step 11: a Gemini account's orders went to Gemini and its balances were read from Binance. It survived because it initially *worked*, and because `internal_calculated_balance` is the **previous run's** exchange figure — so two reads of the same wrong venue always differ by zero and all 176,354 rows read `discrepancy: 0`. **A differential check cannot detect a constant error in its own input.** Fixed by per-account dispatch through `resolveExchangeForAccount`, registry-then-bot-row derived, refusing (not defaulting) an account with no known venue. The old tests asserted only that *an* exchange client was called; the seam now carries the registered exchange (`TestExchangeFactory`, kept separate from the resolvers' label-only `ExchangeFactory`) and 4 tests assert routing, one on the real dispatch path with no network. **Bug 2** — from 07:25:21Z Binance geo-blocked the Worker's egress and every read failed; `reconcile.ts` correctly refused to invent balances (§5.6) but `skipped` went ONLY to `audit_log.details_json` and a `console.log`, so **143 consecutive passes read nothing and alerted nothing**. Note the first diagnosis was **wrong** — the cron never stopped, only its writes did; `audit_log` corrected what `balance_snapshots` implied. Fixed with `auditBlindness`: one standing `critical` alert per incident past `BLIND_AFTER_MS` (16 min), auto-resolved on recovery; explicitly does NOT cover "the cron never fired" (needs an external prober). **Bug 3** — `resume` never cleared `halt_reason`/`halted_at`, which **step 15 recorded as deliberate**; reversed, since the CHECK permits but does not require it and a `running` bot was advertising an already-fixed `MissingAccounts` failure. History moves to the `bot.resumed` audit entry. 14 new tests, all mutation-verified. **Deployment hazard found before deploying and NOT yet resolved:** the stale Binance baselines would make the first corrected pass see a large unexplained `USD`/`BTC` drift, and `balance_drift` is the one kind whose magnitude reaches `severe` → circuit breaker → all bots halted. Remedy is to invalidate the 176,354 wrong-venue rows so the first run takes the "adopt, do not accuse" path — destructive, so **nothing deployed and no live data touched** pending the operator's decision |

and **gross beside net, neither replacing the other**, matching 10.7's per-bot convention, with no bare "P&L" tile anywhere. **Two findings from reading the capital ledger, neither in the brief:** `releaseBotCapital` subtracts a closed bot's allocation from the ledger but **leaves `allocated_capital` on the row** as history, so summing every bot reports returned capital as still committed — an overstatement growing monotonically with every bot ever closed, correct-looking on day one; and `close` cancels orders and releases capital but **does not flatten the position**, so a stopped bot can still hold inventory, which makes **negative idle capital real** and therefore unclamped (amber, not red — red reads as "a loss" everywhere else here). **6 tests, each mutation-verified against the mutation it exists to catch**; the fee test drives real fills through `applyMissedFills` so §5.5's actual conversion runs, asserting `0.25` and **not** `0.325` — the sum that would have added two currencies; the precision test uses fees whose exact total is `1000000000.00000002` where a float gives `1000000000.00000000`, confirmed by running it. **1581 pass** (1575 before). **The frontend fold has NO tests** — the dashboard has no test runner, `vitest.config.ts` runs only the Worker suite, and standing one up is its own deferred step; behaviour was verified off-tree by bundling with esbuild across eight scenarios (all as designed, including negative-idle and asset-grouping), which is **a one-off run, not a regression guard**. Open: `unpricedCount` says how many fills, not how much — quantifying needs a rate the system deliberately refuses to invent |

This does **not** weaken 21.5 requirement 6, and the distinction runs in two directions: **WITHIN** an input fail-closed is untouched (`fetchCandleWindow` still throws six ways, `readAccountExposure` still throws rather than reporting "no concentration" for a read it could not do — nothing here catches those and substitutes a value); **BETWEEN** inputs a failure is **recorded, not propagated**. The failure a whole-bundle throw would cause is specific: **one unreachable venue would erase a concentration flag read successfully from D1** — exactly what 21.4 wants "presented prominently" — and losing a real risk signal because an unrelated fetch failed is not failing closed, it is failing closed on the wrong thing. Value reachable **only** through the `ok` arm (section 5.6's `ExchangeOutcome` discipline, one layer up). **`GatheredInput<T, E>` composes and invents no error vocabulary:** the `failed` arm carries the producing module's **own** error object, so `error.code` reads as it would for a direct caller (`candles_unavailable`, `interval_not_verified`, `bot_list_unreadable`) — a second vocabulary restating those as some flatter `fetch_failed` would drift, and 21.5's duplicated-risk-check rule applies unchanged to duplicated failure descriptions. **Three design notes.** (1) **`Candidate` gets NO envelope**: it exists only because selection succeeded, so selection's failures are refusals that produce no candidate to bundle, and a wrapper would model a state unreachable by construction — made unrepresentable rather than checked for, like `Candidate.sources`; it is asserted to arrive **verbatim by identity** (21.5 req 2). (2) **`threw_unexpectedly` is a distinct state because it is REACHABLE**, backed by a real gap: `fetchCandleWindow` **does not wrap its ports**, so a `Database` or `CandleSource` that throws raw yields something that is not a `CandleWindowError`, and folding it into `failed` would type `error` as `CandleWindowError` while holding a D1 exception; `error` is `unknown`, not `Error`, since a thrown non-`Error` is legal JavaScript. (3) **Concentration's `threw_unexpectedly` is currently UNREACHABLE** — `readAccountExposure`'s catch is total and normalises even a thrown string — and **a test pins it** by driving a `findMany` that throws a bare string, so removing that catch surfaces there rather than in a proposal. **The isolation trace is control flow, not an assertion:** `gatherAgainstExposure` has **no `throw` and no early return**; `foldConcentration`'s parameters **do not include the candle result**, so the coupling is absent by signature and a later edit would have to add an argument to introduce it; `runInput`'s catch is total and returns a value; **no shared mutable state** — no accumulator, no first-error variable; and `gatherCandidateSetData` does one read then a `for` loop that pushes **unconditionally** — no `break`, no `continue`, no predicate. Independent fields with **no shared status and no "first error"**, so candles-failed / concentration-failed / all-succeeded are three distinguishable states. **The news slot is a STATE, not a TODO:** `NEWS_NOT_YET_AVAILABLE`, frozen and shared, in the same discriminant position as every other input's state, with **no `error`, no `failedAt` and no `fetchedAt`** — no request was made and a timestamp would claim one was. "News could not be fetched" invites a retry; the truth is a deliberate pause with three unmet conditions, and **those two sentences must never come from the same branch** — here there is no branch that produces the first. `NewsInput` is a **one-armed union on purpose** (the `ok`/`failed` arms are not pre-declared because nothing can produce them); a vendor landing makes every exhaustive switch stop compiling, which is why the pause lives in the type. **`GatherPorts` holds no news source**, so assembly cannot reach a vendor even by mistake — `candidates.ts`' technique reused. **Every fetch keeps its own timestamp** (21.5 req 4): `candles.value.fetchedAt`, `concentration.value.readAt`, `candidate.sources[]`, and a failed slot's **`failedAt`** (when the failure was *observed*, since no fetch supplied one); `assembledAt` sits beside them, documented as **not one of them**. Tested with a clock that **moves on every read**, and a stub venue answering far from that clock, so a collapse fails there rather than in a proposal. **ONE `bot_instances` read for N candidates** (step 34's design reused), reported on `exposure`; a failed read lands on every candidate's slot as **the same error object by identity** (`toBe`) — accurate because it genuinely was one read, not a duplicated guess — while candles are still fetched per candidate and a **fold** refusal isolates one candidate from its neighbours. **26 tests, 1904 → 1930**, both measured this session by running the suite, not by subtraction; typecheck clean. **10 mutants, 10 caught**, aimed at isolation: rethrow-instead-of-record, candles short-circuiting the fold, a failed read throwing before candles, a failed read reported as **clean** (the one whose survival would be invisible — a clean concentration result is well-formed and hides the exact risk the check exists to surface; the test asserts the **absence of the value**), every throw dressed as the module's own refusal, the news slot rebuilt per bundle, the set dropping or stopping at a failed candidate, a per-candidate re-read, and the quote-asset merge dropped. **M5 and M8 were each caught by a SINGLE test, recorded as thin rather than glossed** — thin in the way that matters, but in both cases the catcher is the test **written for that exact property**, not a bystander that happened to trip. **Six assumptions stated.** Chiefly: **`interval` is REQUIRED with no default** — `VERIFIED_INTERVALS` holds only `"1m"` so a default would be a *correct guess today* and silently wrong the moment it widens, and **the failure mode is why this is not style: each venue spells the wider intervals differently (Gemini writes `"1hr"`/`"6hr"`/`"1day"`), so a wrong interval does not error — it returns correctly-shaped candles of a DIFFERENT duration, which passes every type check and no downstream reader can distinguish**; and **the quote-asset merge is asymmetric between the two functions** — the set-level one reads the operator's own assets off `CandidateSet.trending.quoteAssets` and **merges** (not replaces; `normaliseQuotes` keeps longest-first), while the single-candidate one gets **none**, because a `Candidate` carries no quote assets and inventing some is the silent mis-split `AssessConcentrationOptions.quoteAssets` documents against. Plus: sequential not concurrent (rate budget); `fetchCandleWindow`'s duplicate registry read and `checkTradable` accepted rather than worked around (step 11's rule beats saving a cached call); an exchange/registry disagreement **not** made a new error state since both values are in the bundle. **NAMED OPEN QUESTION — the rate-budget cost of an N-candidate gather.** **No run in this system has ever issued N sequential candle requests as one logical operation** — every fetch to date is one request per operation (a strategy tick, or the single-pair candles endpoint). A general run is exactly that shape: 21.3's 5–10 watchlist coins **plus** a trending pull, so a dozen-plus venue requests in sequence with no pacing of this module's own. Whether that trips **section 5.4's rate budget** — and therefore whether **`candles_unavailable` becomes the NORMAL outcome for the tail of a candidate list rather than an exception** — is **unobserved and unanswerable by stubs** (every test drives an injected `CandleSource`; the tests prove the outcome is *recorded* correctly, not how often it occurs). "Honestly reported and routinely empty" is a design problem, not a success. **To be checked once `gather.ts` has a real caller — an endpoint or the Assess stage — and NOT before:** nothing invokes it in production, so answering it now means standing up a temporary caller and inventing the request shape, candidate count and interval the real caller will decide, **re-deriving exactly the conditions this step correctly deferred**; the number would have to be redone and would be worse than none because it would look like an answer. **No live check for this step, judged unnecessary and agreed independently:** no new read/write/wire-format boundary exists, the three inputs' live behaviour is already proven (29, 33, 31, 34's 2026-08-10T20:35:36Z real-data run), and isolation is a structural claim about control flow that a live run tests no better than the mutants do. **Both suite runs report `Errors 1 error`** — the pre-existing `vitest-pool` teardown "Worker exited unexpectedly", present in the clean-tree baseline, attributable to no test file, **no test failed in either run**; the same one step 34 recorded. **Deferred:** no endpoint; **no judgement of any kind** (no ranking, scoring, filtering or refusing); no "what failed" summary helper, since shaping it before Stage 4 exists shapes it to a caller nobody has written; no concurrency; no news vendor; **`gather.ts` has no non-test caller**, because the thing that would call it is the proposal pipeline. **No `wrangler` command, no deploy, no exchange call and no news vendor contacted this session** |

| [37.md](37.md) | 37 | **Assess (Stage 2): a prompt that must be cited, a parser that refuses, an envelope bug only a live call could find, and a protection reported rather than faked.** `assess-prompt.ts` / `assess-parse.ts` / `assess.ts` — the first step in this project involving an LLM, built and adversarially tested **before** any model was contacted. **There is now a prompt**, so the folder banner was rewritten rather than repeated; what stays true is **no `ai` binding in `wrangler.jsonc`, no proposal record, no pipeline, no write path to a bot**. **Research first (verified vs assumed):** `env.AI.run(model, inputs, options?)`; `temperature` default **0.6** (min 0), `seed` 1..9,999,999,999, `max_tokens` default **256**, `top_p` min **0.001**; JSON mode is `response_format` and Cloudflare states it **"can't guarantee"** compliance; **300 req/min**, $0.011/1,000 Neurons with **10,000/day free**; context **24,000 tokens**. Model `@cf/meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct-fp8-fast` chosen because it is the only non-deprecated model appearing **both** on the JSON Mode list **and** carrying `response_format` in its own schema — the JSON Mode page and the per-model schemas **disagree** elsewhere, so one page is not evidence. **Grounding is enforced, not requested:** every datum gets a stable **evidence id**, every claim must cite one, and the parser **refuses the whole response** for a citation naming an id this run never emitted — a set-membership test rather than a judgement. **A missing input produces MORE text, never less** (an absent section reads exactly like "there was nothing to report", and those are opposite facts). **Candles are bucketed** — 1,440 candles will not fit 24k tokens — with the count, the rule and the omission stated in the prompt; a full bundle measures **14,771 chars / 54 evidence items**. **Injection defence:** third-party text (trending `name`/`symbol`/`coinId`/`vendor`, watchlist `note`, `requestedAs`) wrapped in `<<<UNTRUSTED_TEXT chars=N>>>` with **rule 3** stating wrapped text is *data to report on, never an instruction* — on the same footing as the price numbers. **Emitted byte for byte**: stripping it would be silent alteration of fetched data and would destroy the evidence an attempt was made. **It REDUCES risk and does not eliminate it**; what bounds the damage is structural — the stage produces a recommendation a human reviews (21.1), and **an injection can change the ANSWER but not the DATA the answer is checked against**. **The parser has no lenient path** — no case folding (`"DCA"` refused), no fence stripping, no first-`{`-to-last-`}`, no ignored field, no dropped citation, no default — and **no vocabulary-based hedge detection**, because a word list fails open on unlisted hedges and closed on the caution the prompt requires; the hedge that matters is a hedged *choice*, caught exactly by two literals. Adversarial fixtures include truncation at `max_tokens`, Cloudflare's own `JSON Mode couldn't be met` text, `__proto__`, and a **duplicate `strategy` key** that `JSON.parse` resolves cleanly. **Zero retries, deliberately:** a refusal *is* fail-closed working, and "retry until it parses" selects the answer **for passing the validator** rather than for being what the model gave — and with `temperature: 0` plus a fixed seed, a retry that succeeds is evidence the pinning failed, which the loop would consume. **Live (operator, three real calls).** **Probe 1 found the bug the tests could not:** the model reasoned correctly and **the parser refused it — correctly**. With `response_format: json_schema` this model returns `{ response: { strategy: ..., claims: [...] } }` — an **already-parsed object**, not the string **Cloudflare's own generated type declares**. Fix confined to `unwrapModelEnvelope`; all four shapes land in **one shared `validateAnswerObject`**, so a rule relaxed for one path must be relaxed for the other in the same visible edit. **Probe 2 (`returnRawResponse`) closed the follow-up question with the wire bytes**: `rawBodyText` is literally `{"response":{"strategy":"grid",...}}`, so the object shape is real to the wire and not an SDK artefact. **The duplicate-key theatre finding:** re-stringifying the parsed object and scanning it would be **fake protection** — `JSON.stringify` of an already-parsed object can never contain a duplicate, so the scan returns null forever while reading like a guard, and **a check that cannot fail is worse than an absent one because only the absent one is visible**. Reported instead, per response, as **`duplicateKeyCheck: "performed" | "unavailable_transport_parsed"`** on both result types, so an audit record says which guarantees actually held. Two mutants pin it, including the theatre version itself. **Stated at the width of the evidence:** both real calls produced clean, well-grounded, correctly-cited `"grid"` answers that **correctly reported missing news as a limitation** — a positive early signal and **two data points, not a compliance rate**. **NOT a determinism test:** the two runs saw different `candles.earliest_close`/`latest_close` (new candles arrived between them), so inputs were not identical; **`temperature: 0` + fixed seed on truly identical input is untested**, and needs the same bundle fed twice. **Latency is a RANGE, not a figure: 13,540 / 20,326 / 10,614 ms** (~2× spread). The earlier one-point "~2.5 min for 12 candidates" is widened to **two to four minutes in Assess calls alone**, and Stage 3 is a **second** call per candidate — so **whether Assess can run synchronously in an HTTP response or must be queued is an OPEN QUESTION, to settle before Stage 3, not after**. **OPEN PROBLEM, structural:** declaring the `ai` binding makes `vitest-pool-workers` open a **remote proxy session at config-parse time**, and the suite reports **0 tests / 75 errors**; `vitest.config.ts` pins `environment: "testnet"`, so **the binding cannot be declared for deploys without also declaring it for tests**. **production `wrangler.jsonc` is untouched**, a deliberate separate decision; the dry run proves **manifest inclusion, not a successful deploy**; and the pool's gate was read from **0.18.6 specifically**, with version and line numbers recorded in the config comment so an upgrade can re-check rather than re-derive. **Still NOT done, explicitly: `worker-configuration.d.ts` has NOT been regenerated**, so `Env` has **no `AI` field** and **no application code can reference `env.AI` and typecheck** — the pinning test casts locally for exactly this reason, and **`npm run cf-typegen` is a real prerequisite for `envAssessModel`** (it rewrites a 551KB tracked file), deliberately out of a step scoped to the binding conflict. **`envAssessModel` itself is still unbuilt**; | [40.md](40.md) | 40 | **The assess endpoint — gather → assess → response for ONE named candidate, verified end to end on real market data, on a sandbox whose data is not a real market.** `GET /api/accounts/:label/assess?pair=&interval=`, Access-gated, `envelope.ts` status mapping reused, plus `evidenceItemView`/`citedClaimView`/`assessResultView` and an injectable `assessModel` port on `ApiContext` **so no test can fall through to a paid vendor**. **6 files modified, 0 new, +687/−0.** **ONE candidate deliberately** — `entryPoint` fixed to `named` with no batch parameter to grow one by accident, because at 10.5–20.3 s per assessment a watchlist run is a **different design problem**, not a parameter on this one. **Duration settled BEFORE building, from Cloudflare's own docs:** HTTP-triggered Workers have **no wall-clock duration limit** (*"no hard limit … as long as the client remains connected"*), and the limit that does exist — CPU time, 30 s default on Paid — **excludes waiting on network requests** (*"Waiting on network requests … does not count toward CPU time"*). A model call is almost entirely that wait; the endpoint's own CPU work is milliseconds and it issues ~6–8 of 10,000 allowed subrequests. **One real caveat, quoted:** a runtime update gives in-flight requests a **30-second grace period**, which a 20 s request could collide with — rare, non-deterministic, presenting as a dropped connection. **ASSUMED, not verified: Workers Paid**, inferred from Durable Object usage; on Free the 10 ms CPU limit would likely break the prompt builder. **The failure rule: the model is asked only when the question can be grounded; every other gather failure is REPORTED, not propagated.** No candle window → the request fails via `assessCandidate`'s `no_price_history` precondition, **unbypassed** (mutant caught). **Failed concentration or the paused news slot → the assessment RUNS**, with the gap stated to the model as missing and returned verbatim in the bundle — refusing there would be the over-propagation `gather.ts` exists to avoid, losing a grounded assessment because an unrelated D1 read failed (mutant caught). A bad model answer is a **502 carrying the parser's own code**, mapped by class in one place rather than twenty near-identical `STATUS_BY_CODE` rows. **ATTRIBUTION CORRECTED HERE:** the *"no assessment was attempted"* improvement was reported as step 39's and **is this step's** — `git` shows `handlers.ts` at +178/−0, uncommitted, with HEAD at 39's `envAssessModel` commit which touched no endpoint. `assessCandidate` knows only `no_price_history` because at that layer the reason genuinely is not known; at the endpoint it **is** — the candle slot carries the producing module's own error — so the underlying code and status are surfaced (`interval_not_verified`→400, `candles_unavailable`→502) **with the precondition unchanged and still the thing that refused**. Prompted by a unit test expecting 400 that got 503, and now confirmed under a real call. **Live, operator-run, whole chain (the FIRST real end-to-end operator verification of Assess — every prior live check exercised one stage in isolation):** strategy **grid**, **4 claims**, every citation a real emitted id, **`range_pct` = 1.14868489% reconciles EXACTLY by hand against the real high/low in the same response**, news `not_yet_available` **and named as a real limitation in the model's own claim** (rule 5 working on real inputs, unprompted by any fixture), `envelope_object` + `unavailable_transport_parsed` **matching all four prior probes exactly** (five consistent observations of a shape Cloudflare's generated type still declares differently), latency **11,470 ms**. C makes it judge **which kind a citation is and whether that kind is permitted here**, manufacturing a new *"right id, wrong kind"* refusal class **with no observed failure behind it**, and failing its own first case since a parameter may legitimately rest on a datum **and** the assessment at once. **The core reason for A is OVER-FITTING: two data points, from two live runs.** A rule written now fits *what has been seen* rather than *what needs solving*, and its likely failure is not strictness but a third shape arriving that it neither catches nor is obviously wrong for. Supporting: **the mechanism did not fail** (a change would alter a working safety mechanism in response to a non-defect); **its value is that it is mechanical** (every semantic rule erodes the determinism step 37 chose set-membership for); and **the distinction is genuinely useful, just not HERE** — nobody argued the three kinds are equivalent, only about which layer should know. **⚠ SCOPED TO THE PARSER/VALIDATION LAYER AND NOTHING ELSE.** Read as *"all citations are interchangeable"* this entry would be **actively harmful — the opposite of what was concluded**. **⚠ A REQUIREMENT PLACED ON STAGE 4 (Explain, not yet built), not a suggestion and not something it may rediscover independently: Stage 4's presentation layer MUST be designed knowing a citation may point to (a) real fetched data, (b) an absence marker, or (c) a prior stage's own claim, and MUST describe these differently rather than rendering all three as interchangeable "here is the source" citations.** (b) and (c) are **not weaker citations and must not be hidden or demoted — the requirement is to stop them reading as (a)**, because a reviewer who cannot tell that a parameter rests on a non-observation or on another model's judgement is shown a proposal **that looks better grounded than it is**, and 21.1 puts that reviewer there as the only part of the loop that can catch a bad upstream judgement. **The classification needs NO parser change — which is what makes the decision coherent rather than merely deferred:** `EvidenceItem.source` already carries it (`assessment.*` → (c); `news` and `*.error` → (b); everything else → (a)), so **option B would have added a field to say what `source` already says**. **stated in four places including the rendered footer** (*"If you close this tab, this proposal is gone"*) so a working Stage 4 is not mistaken for req 5 landing. **What it deliberately does NOT do**, each refused for a stated reason: **no re-verification of citations** (duplicates step 42), **no re-validation of parameters** (*a second implementation of a risk check drifts, and the copy that drifts is the one nobody is watching*), **no model call**, and **no approve button or prefilled create-bot link** — the one-click bridge is the affordance that turns "the human approves" into a rubber stamp. **Transport is pasted responses, not a trigger**, argued as a **21.6** question: every research endpoint is curl-only, a `/derive` costs a real inference and 41,712–88,480 ms of model time has been measured across the three call-chains so far, and the resubmission projection is new client BEHAVIOUR not presentation — **recorded as a judgement call, not an obvious one**. **Suite 2,377 → 2,395** (82/83 → 83/84 files, 0 failures): `citations.test.ts` (+18) **drives the backend's REAL prompt builders over every outcome state**, because a rule about `EvidenceItem.source` tested against hand-typed strings **pins the dashboard's belief about the backend rather than the backend**. **Mutation: 4 mutants, 4 caught**, each grepped for after application before its result was trusted (the rule from 10.14, 41, 42). **⚠ NONE of the eight components is tested** — no jsdom, no testing-library; a bot config has none, so a clone would supply them empty and the verdict would resolve to **`unknown`** about a config that is **exactly complete** and was never AI-generated — **a degraded result presented as a real one, which is precisely what that module's fail-closed design exists to prevent.** **(2)** one fail-closed rule would become a **disjunction with a real fall-through arm** where neither id is present. **(3)** that decoder feeds the **capital-committing** proposal-to-bot path, which now carries **ZERO changed lines** — `proposalPrefill.ts` was not edited at all. **The real cost, stated:** a second decoder **duplicates the field mapping**, and that is the error-prone part rather than an incidental one — grid and DCA **both** have a parameter literally called `stopLossPct` going to two different form inputs, and a mapping right for one and wrong for the other **typechecks, renders, and creates a bot with no stop-loss where a human saw one**. **Two safeguards:** the wire field lists are **IMPORTED** from the shared authority both decoders use (already pinned element-for-element against the backend's own lists by an existing test, so a new parameter reaches both encoders or neither), and **a test feeds one parameter set through BOTH decoders and asserts the resulting fields are byte-identical**. Also: the clone decoder refuses a URL carrying **both** ids **inside itself**, not by call order, so a future reordering cannot silently regress. **THREE FINDINGS FROM THE API RESPONSE: (1)** two allocated-capital figures can **technically diverge** — the D1 row, which `resizeBotCapital` keeps current, versus the DO's own `config.allocatedCapital`, written once at creation and never updated. They agree on every bot today because **no route currently reaches `resizeBotCapital`**; latent, not observed. **The clone uses the ROW's figure, deliberately: that is what the operator is actually looking at on screen when they press Clone.** **The fixtures were built with the two values DIFFERENT (`4000` vs `3500`) so the assertion can only pass if the correct source was used** — built the realistic way, with them agreeing, the test would have passed either way and proved nothing. **(2)** `exchange` is **deliberately NOT carried**: it is read-only on the form, derived from the account registry, and `POST /api/bots` **rejects any mismatch against that registry** — carrying it would be a value nothing consumes that could contradict the source of truth. **(3)** **orphaned bots** (row present, no DO state) have **no config to copy**, so no honest link can exist — **the one place literal "no gating" met a case where a working link genuinely cannot be built. NOT a status gate**, and orphanhood is orthogonal to status (a test iterates all four statuses plus archived and asserts a link **is** offered). **The spirit was preserved:** the control **still renders unconditionally on every bot, orphans included** — it states the reason in place rather than silently hiding, deliberately breaking `LiquidateAction`'s "don't render an invalid action" rule the way `ArchiveAction`'s held-position case already does, because **an operator told the control is on every bot reads its absence as a broken page.** **A SELF-CAUGHT SAFETY-TEST CONFLICT:** new code tripped the **existing** guard enforcing *"only one function may attach a `proposalId` anywhere in this codebase"* — a key-table entry naming the parameter purely so the decoder could **detect and refuse** it. It never wrote it. **The fix was to remove the conflicting shape from the NEW code, not to weaken the guard**, replacing the object member with a bare `const`. Recorded as the correct instinct: the tempting carve-out was narrow and the occurrence genuinely benign, but **a guard that must distinguish harmless occurrences from real ones is a guard with an exception list**, and the next case gets judged against a precedent instead of a rule. **An established safety rule should not bend for new code; new code should conform to it.** The guard was also **extended** — three new files added to its navigation-path set, with the extra reason that this path's source is a **live bot** rather than a stored document, so its worst failure would be **touching a running bot** as a side effect of copying its numbers. **VERIFICATION: 33 new tests** in one new file — full DCA round trip (incl. the row-vs-config capital assertion), full grid round trip (incl. **a `null` optional decoding as genuinely "unset" rather than "incomplete"**), **five** interference assertions proving the two flows cannot collide in either direction (incl. **a request carrying BOTH ids refused entirely**), **ten** fail-closed cases (missing source, whitespace-only id, missing/bogus strategy, **missing fields named as incomplete rather than guessed**, the `Boolean("false")` parsing trap, junk passed through **verbatim** for the form's validator, and the orphan/malformed-config cases), plus **one sanity test proving the negatives aren't passing vacuously**; **2 tests added** to the existing navigation-guard suite. **Suite: 3050 (107/108) → 3085 (108/109), +35 matching 33 + 2 exactly, +1 file, zero regressions**; starting figure checked against entry **65**'s close rather than assumed. Both typechecks clean (Worker and dashboard); a real dashboard build succeeds — **121 modules transformed** — which matters more than usual because **no test here can import a `.tsx`**, so the two new components are covered by typecheck, that build and the source guard and nothing else. The two new components have **never been rendered**. The capital divergence is **latent, not observed**. No backend change was made or needed. **Nothing committed as of writing.** |

Every path that empties a ladder (`#gridExit`, `liquidatePosition`, and the `#cancelOpenOrders` sweep **every `#halt` runs**) nulls the slots and leaves the latch alone; `resume()` never touches the ladder. **Result: empty slots + `placed: true` ⇒ `hold` on every tick, forever** — a bot genuinely running, genuinely watching the market, permanently unable to place a single order, with nothing detecting or correcting it. **NOT NARROW:** `#halt` is reached from manual halt, reconciliation, the kill switch, the circuit breaker, `order_rejected` and `unhandled_error` — **the ordinary halt/resume lifecycle was the disabling mechanism.** Surfaced by two real testnet bots, **`bot-qgo39d`** (liquidated, resumed) and **`bot-gvtr1a`** (whose ladder was already empty *before* entry 77's detector ran — the detector inherited an inert bot, it did not create one). **THE LESSON: the defect was written down correctly FOUR times before anyone recognised it** — twice in unrelated sessions weeks earlier (`ApplyMissedFillsAction.tsx`, `7d7ad72`, 2026-07-31; `findings.ts`, `89044b8`, 2026-08-05), once tonight as a *premise* in entry 74's own test comment, once in entry 77's PART 0. **THE FIX:** keep the latch for its real, narrower job (driving the throttled-partial-placement retry) and add an independent **five-part** `vacantLadder` — every slot empty, nothing held, **no order resting**, no exit live, no replacement queued — gated as `!placed || vacant`. **`openOrderIds` is one of the five** because `#gridExit` clears every rung while `#cancelOpenOrders` retains unconfirmed cancellations: without it a bot would place a fresh ladder **on top of an order that may still be genuinely live** — entry 74's hazard from the opposite direction. **REJECTED: reset-at-each-clear-site**, forward-only and **could never help either real bot**, both already carrying `placed: true` in permanent storage. **REJECTED: rebuild inside `resume()` — it would have PARTIALLY UNDONE tonight's 3a**, which deliberately aims an interrupted resume at a state where nothing can act; placing real orders there would leave live exchange orders under a bot that believes itself halted and will never poll or cancel them — *one fix's design surfacing a real hazard in a second, reasonable-sounding fix.* **THE GATE REORDER IS LOAD-BEARING:** placement moved BELOW the three risk exits, because `#gridExit` clears the slots but **not `realizedGross`** — a bot resumed still past its take-profit or stop-loss would rebuild its whole ladder and re-trigger the same exit next tick, **real orders placed and cancelled every cycle, forever**; the old comment argued only that placement-first was *harmless*, never *necessary*, and that stopped being true once the gate could fire on a bot with real history. **SECOND EXPOSURE CLOSED:** a start with nothing placeable latched `placed: true` having placed **zero** orders — now guarded. **DETECTOR RESPECIFIED HONESTLY:** a reconciliation finding was rejected because the `meaningful` tier **halts the very bot the fix exists to revive** and `minor` never alerts; built instead as a standing, self-resolving alert on the bot's own price path, at the single site that knows both that a rebuild ran and whether it placed anything — avoiding the split ownership `alerts/standing.ts` warns against. **MUTATION: 11 mutants, ALL KILLED** — M4 (gate restored to first, 9 failed), M6 (vacant disjunct removed — the original bug, 7 failed) and M7 (`!placed` removed — throttle retry lost) together proving neither disjunct covers the other; **three survived their first run (M9/M10/M11) and were correctly resolved as genuine DEFENCE IN DEPTH, not gaps** — two of the three conditions provably cannot occur independently (an exit sell and a queued replacement both imply held base, which already refuses), **proven by exporting `gridOutstanding` and testing all three against its real contract**, with one end-to-end test added for the one condition that IS reachable. **SELF-CAUGHT:** two PRE-EXISTING tests were found to have been asserting the wrong thing all along — their `placedLadder()` helper built the exact dead-ladder shape this bug is about, so they exercised the rebuild path while claiming to test `hold`; fixed with a `workingLadder()` helper. Two of this session's own test-authoring mistakes recorded (wrong order grabbed in a fixture; missing coverage for the outstanding composition). **SUITE: 3227 → 3265 (+38 tests, +1 file), zero failures**, `Errors 1` unchanged (the known teardown artifact); **the full PRE-EXISTING 3227 were confirmed passing with the implementation in place and before a single new test was added**, specifically to prove the reorder introduced no hidden regression. **[OPERATOR] LIVE CONFIRMATION of `bot-gvtr1a`** (this session made no live calls): `heldQuantity` reads **exactly zero** and `placed` reads **`true`** — closing the one conditional the design could not settle and confirming the diagnosis is the latch, not a different problem wearing the same symptoms; its alert history separately shows **the exact drift → converging-halt → both-resolved sequence 3a/3b/3c was built to produce**, observed live a second time tonight. **NOT DEPLOYED, NOT EXERCISED LIVE.** Both bots are **EXPECTED, not observed**, to self-correct on their first post-deploy tick. The human-triggered "force a rebuild" action (option d) was designed and **deliberately not built** — not needed while both bots are flat, recorded as available if one is ever found holding a position in this state. |
| [80.md](80.md) | 80 | **A cosmetic status alert could halt a live trading bot — the severity inversion entry 79 shipped by accident. NOT COMMITTED**; two files modified (`bot-instance.ts` +73/−25, `grid-bot-instance.test.ts` +158/−2), plus this row; `HEAD` still `8264456`. **THE INVERSION:** entry 79's `grid_ladder_vacant` alert exists purely to REASSURE — it tells the operator that a grid bot idle below its own range is correctly waiting, not broken — yet it made its own D1 read (`alerts.findMany`, via `raiseStandingAlert`) on **every price tick, once per minute, indefinitely**, from inside the `try` whose only handler is `#haltOnUnexpected`. **A cosmetic status report could stop real trading, while the actual trading logic in the same pass was entirely unaffected by whatever went wrong** — `gridDecide` had already run and the `PipelineResult` was already computed before the check was even reached. **LIVE:** `bot-gnqel3` halted 2026-08-25 21:07 on `unhandled_error: Error: D1_ERROR: D1 DB storage operation exceeded timeout which caused object to be reset.` — healthy, correctly idle, no position, no open orders. A prior investigation (referenced, not re-derived) established the string appears nowhere in this codebase outside test fixtures and that the D1 layer has no retry/timeout logic: a **raw, transient Cloudflare platform error, unrelated to this codebase and to the operator's own network**. **THIS DOES NOT FIX THE D1 TIMEOUT** — a one-off external event — only this codebase's RESPONSE to any such failure on this one advisory path. **FIX:** the check now fails silently; the pass continues exactly as if it had never been attempted, and it retries next tick anyway. **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE BRIEF, both found by tracing:** (1) there are **TWO** D1 operations at risk, not one — the raise branch AND the resolve branch each make their own `alerts.findMany`, and wrapping only the first would have left a RECOVERING bot exposed to the identical halt; (2) the fix is at the **call site, NOT in the shared `raiseStandingAlert`** — that function also serves reconciliation's **critical** findings (2 direct sites) and, through `#raiseStanding`'s 6 call sites, the **poll-blindness detector** (`poll_blind`, `poll_blind_escalated`), while `resolveClearedStandingAlerts` has 3 other non-test sites including the poll's own resolve half; **silencing there would have quietly defanged alerts that are SUPPOSED to be loud**. `alerts/standing.ts` untouched. **SCOPE JUDGMENT:** the WHOLE advisory method is wrapped, not just its D1 calls — a `#state()` read sits in the same function and would be equally pointless to halt over, the trading decision for that tick having already been made. **DIAGNOSTICS:** the failure path logs bot id, full error and **stack** (`#haltOnUnexpected` records only `name: message`, which is what made the original slow to place); logged and NOT alerted, since an alert row is a D1 write — the very thing that failed. **Explicitly NOT a rework of `#haltOnUnexpected`**, unchanged for every other case. **4 TESTS:** the live incident reproduced verbatim with the real platform string (bot confirmed still `running`/`placed_initial_ladder`, and the alert raised cleanly on the NEXT tick); the RESOLVE branch (proving correction 1); the ordinary path unregressed; and — **named as a SCOPE-PROOF, not a regression guard** — an unrelated genuine trading failure (`orders.insert`) in the same pass **still correctly halts**. **MUTATION:** fix reverted to its exact pre-fix state — **tests 1 and 2 FAILED with `expected 'halted' to be 'running'`, reproducing the live incident precisely**; tests 3 and 4 passed with and without the fix **by design**, since neither tests the `catch`. **SUITE: 3265 → 3269 (+4 tests, 0 new files), zero failures**, `Errors 1` unchanged (the known teardown artifact); **the measured baseline matched entry 79's own recorded figure exactly** (117 files (118) / 3265), an independent cross-check that nothing drifted between sessions. **EMPIRICAL CONFIRMATION:** the investigation's code-read claim that this alert's D1 read was the ONLY D1 operation in an idle grid bot's whole price-update pass is now **measured** — with that one call forced to throw, the rest of the pass completed normally, impossible if any other D1 call existed on that path. **NOT DEPLOYED, NOT EXERCISED LIVE.** Whether `bot-gnqel3` currently holds an open `grid_ladder_vacant` row is **NOT checked** and left for the operator; whether it can now survive a similar blip while idle is **EXPECTED, not observed**. |
| [81.md](81.md) | 81 | **The PriceFeed compute-duration leak — feeds that stayed awake with nothing listening. NOT COMMITTED**; six files modified (`price-feed.ts`, `price-feed.test.ts` **+344**, `handlers.ts` **+62**, `api.test.ts` **+92**, `index.ts` **+5**, `halt.ts`), **+838/−44** overall, plus this row; `HEAD` still `7bc3aae`. **THE SYMPTOM:** the Jul 22–Aug 21 cycle billed **$12.65, of which $12.50 was Durable Objects Compute Duration**, landing on a **single day (Aug 12)** — an artifact of the **400,000 GB-second allowance** only producing a dollar figure the day the running total crosses it, NOT a spike; usage had accrued invisibly all month. Confirmed still active next cycle: **284.44k GB-s in 6 days (~47.4k/day) = ~4.4 objects resident against 2 legitimately running `(exchange, pair)` pairs**, projecting to cross the allowance again **~Aug 29**. A `PriceFeed` **cannot hibernate while connected** (step 14: outbound sockets do not hibernate), so every resident feed bills continuously. **ROOT CAUSE, PRIMARY:** `#fanOut` used `Promise.allSettled` and only ALERTED on a rejected delivery — it never unsubscribed a permanently-failing subscriber, and the comment at that site deferred the case to *"section 9 reconciliation's concern"* — **reconciliation that was never written** (`grep` over `src/reconciliation/` for `subscriber`/`unsubscribe`/`PRICE_FEED` returns nothing). Confirmed via D1: **six halted/stopped bots** (`bot-328qgw`, `bot-3trlgb`, `bot-4xcq8p`, `bot-9wzfci`, `bot-xs0ufw`, `prop-live-1`) **still held live subscriber rows and were still failing delivery**, so `unsubscribe`'s zero-check never fired, `stopFeed` (its **only** production caller) never ran, and `stopFeed` held the **only `deleteAlarm` in the file** — every other path re-armed, forever. **ROOT CAUSE, SECONDARY (race):** `stopFeed`'s own `socket.close()` dispatched a `close` event wired to `#onSocketClosed` → `#scheduleReconnect`, which re-arms **unconditionally**, while `deleteAlarm()` was `stopFeed`'s **last** statement — so a feed tearing itself down asked, through its own teardown, to be reconnected; and because `stopFeed` reset only the watermark and **left `#state.config` set**, the stray alarm passed `#ensureConnected`'s guard and reconnected **for real**. **THE FIX:** durable per-subscriber `consecutive_failures` column pruning at **`MAX_FANOUT_FAILURES = 5`** (~5 min of proven-dead delivery; **durable not in-memory precisely because a feed whose only subscriber is dead is the one most likely to be evicted**, resetting an in-memory counter fastest in the case it exists to catch; the constructor is the migration point, via a guarded `ALTER TABLE`); a **`#stopped` latch** plus **listener removal before `close()`**; `stopFeed` reordered **latch → drop socket ref → close → clear config+watermark → persist → `deleteAlarm` LAST**; **`#armAlarm(Timestamp | null)`** with a disarm branch mirroring `bot-instance.ts` (**the old non-nullable signature was itself part of the bug** — every path could arm, none could stand down); `alarm()` reads the registry **first** as a backstop (empty ⇒ `stopFeed()`, **regardless of how it got that way**), while **a halted, unreachable or failing subscriber still COUNTS** so a live bot is never silently cut off; and a new `status()` RPC behind **`GET /api/price-feeds`** with a computed `leaking` flag. **FOUR DEVIATIONS, STATED NOT INFERRED:** (1) the brief's `#stopping` flag *cleared at the end of `stopFeed`* **would not have closed the race** — `deleteAlarm()` IS the last statement, so the flag would already be down when a late `close` lands; implemented as a **persistent latch cleared only by a deliberate `startFeed`**. (2) **"List live feeds" is not achievable** — **Durable Object namespaces are not enumerable by the platform** and `idFromName` is one-way; built instead as *derive every feed that COULD exist from `DISTINCT (exchange, pair)` over `bot_instances`, then probe each with `status()`* — **a real scope reduction from "list what is live" to "list what is possible and check each," recorded as a stated limitation in the route's own docblock**, the same disclosure stance as `CandleWindow.truncated` (29, 37, 44) and `NewsInput = NewsNotYetAvailable` (35). (3) `#alert` gained an optional `botInstanceId`, fixing the pre-existing hardcoded `bot_instance_id: null` on **every** PriceFeed alert — justified by a **deleted-bot FK violation that cannot occur** (bots are archived, never removed; one `DELETE` route exists, for the watchlist) — **an INTENTIONAL side effect, not a bug fix**: these rows now surface on `GET /api/bots/:id`, and the Discord cooldown key moves **shared → per-bot**, so five failing bots means **five pings instead of one**, bounded by the new prune cap; `halt.ts`'s *"excluded twice over"* comment was **made false by this change and rewritten** (the `source` filter is now load-bearing alone). (4) an **FK-failure fallback** in `#alert` — retry with a null id rather than propagate — because a throw there happens **inside `#forwardClosed` before the watermark advances**, turning one constraint failure into an **endlessly-retried candle**; *attribute when possible, degrade to null rather than fail*, the same disclosed-degradation shape as `GatheredInput`'s three-state envelope (45), **written into the docblock so it is findable by someone reading `#alert` cold**. **NOT FIXED, DELIBERATELY DEFERRED, AND NOT TO BE ABSORBED INTO "the leak is fixed":** `price_feed_blind` **has no resolve path anywhere** (all four resolving paths filter by `source` or an explicit type list; none covers `price-feed:*`) — **two 20-day-old unresolved rows** for `gemini:BTCUSD` and `gemini:DOGEUSD` proved to be **stale bookkeeping, not a live outage**, but cost real investigation time to rule out; and **a fan-out failure permanently drops that candle** (the watermark advances in `#forwardClosed` **regardless of delivery outcome**, and the dedup guard then suppresses it forever) while **the system has no per-tick record anywhere, D1 or DO storage** — `lastPrice`/`lastPriceAt` are a **two-field overwrite** with no history, counter or audit entry — so **whether a dropped candle mattered to a running bot is not determinable retrospectively by anyone**. **Whether skipped candles are safe to keep skipping is a distinct question needing its own entry.** **VERIFICATION:** suite **3269 → 3281 (+12)**, 117 files (118), `Errors 1`, zero failures; **the measured baseline matched entry 80's recorded final exactly**; `Errors 1` and the `close timed out` message **verified present and identical in the stashed baseline before anything was attributed to this change**; `tsc --noEmit` 0; dashboard build clean. **NOT VERIFIED:** **no deploy, no production billing number yet** — follow-up is to check Compute Duration **~24h after deploy**, expecting **~21k GB-s/day** (the two-legitimate-feed baseline) against **~47.4k/day** pre-fix; **until that number is in hand this entry does not close the issue**. Whether the six existing leaked feeds self-heal on deploy is **EXPECTED, not confirmed**, and **the `ALTER TABLE` branch that matters in production — an existing one-column table — is the one the local suite cannot construct**. |

It **corrects nothing** and is **not scheduled**. **⚠ ONE DELIBERATE API BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, called out separately in PART 0: a second `POST /api/bots/:id/close` now returns `200` with `action: "already_stopped"` instead of `409 bot_already_stopped`** — `#closePass` tolerates that ONE ledger code and continues to cleanup, because every retry previously died on the release line before reaching the steps a partial close had skipped. **No capital moves twice**: the ledger's conditional update is untouched and exactly one `capital.released` row is ever written; `release_exceeds_allocated`, `unknown_bot_instance` and `allocation_conflict` still propagate. **`bot-xs0ufw` deliberately left untouched** as the real-world test: re-halting it after deploy should be what finally cleans it up. **3298/3298 tests pass** (baseline 3281/3281), `tsc --noEmit` clean, dashboard build clean. |
| [83.md](83.md) | 83 | **The trailing-stop strategy — a third strategy, and a migration that passed every local test and failed three times in production.
