Planned in advance as spec section 22 and built against it; section 22 rewritten from *"PLANNED, NOT YET BUILT"* into a record of what exists. **The strategy:** one parameter (`trailPct`, bounds **1–20% PROVISIONAL** per 22.5), a single entry sized by `allocatedCapital`, exit when `price <= max(entry, highWaterMark) × (1 − trailPct)`, and the exit **halts** rather than completing a cycle. **Decisions:** `trailing_stop` over shorter names, because `OrderState`'s persisted `partially_filled` shows `grid`/`dca`'s brevity was coincidence, not convention; the single-entry-sized-by-allocation reading made **explicit in 22.2** rather than left implicit, since it is load-bearing in `orderSizesOf`, `plannedSpendOf` and the validator; `exitKind: "trailing_stop"` + `trailing_stop_reached` following DCA's exact `_reached` pairing, and added to `#halt`'s `positiveExit` so the strategy's **intended** exit alerts `info` and not `critical`; the exit routed away from `#completeCycle` because `autoRestart` was traced to **do nothing at all** (the restart is emergent) and a restart would be a **second entry**, which 22.2 decision 4 excludes; `highWaterMark` declared **optional, not `Money | null`**, because it is additive to a shape live bots already hold on disk — entry **69**'s precedent (`unmodelledBase`, `exitKind`, `pendingReplacements`) — with the ratchet in exactly one place and `decide()` **reading** it, never recomputing; and **no** new `CHECK`, because `stop_loss_pct` is already `NOT NULL CHECK (> 0)` globally and the real hazard (`stop_loss_pct` and `trailPct` being one quantity with two homes) is a **decoder** concern per 21.5 requirement 3, settled in `createTrailingStop`. **⚠ THE MIGRATION POSTMORTEM, the centre of this entry:** `0010` rebuilds two tables (SQLite cannot `ALTER` a `CHECK`), passed local D1 at **17 commands zero errors** and passed miniflare and `schema.test.ts` — and **failed remote D1 three consecutive times**, auto-rolled-back with zero data loss. **Not the PRAGMA** (D1 documents `foreign_keys = OFF` as unsupported and `defer_foreign_keys` as the only mechanism — it was used correctly) and **not bad data** (`foreign_key_check` on a real export is **empty**). The cause: SQLite's deferred FK enforcement is a **violation COUNTER, not a data re-check**. `DROP TABLE bot_instances` orphaned **1,099 real child rows** (478 alerts, 345 audit_log, 138 trades, 134 orders, 4 proposals) across 30 bots, incrementing the counter once each; `ALTER TABLE ... RENAME` restores the data but **never decrements the counter**. Reproduced locally against a real testnet export, and the proof is the finding: immediately before the failing `COMMIT`, **30 rows present and a LEFT JOIN across every child showing ZERO orphans** — **consistent data, refused commit**. **⚠ No locally-runnable test could ever have caught it**: with empty tables `DROP` orphans nothing, the counter stays 0, and the bug is structurally invisible — **stated as standing guidance for any future rebuild of a table with real FK dependents, a property of the environment rather than a lapse in the tests**, and the third time this codebase has recorded a defect testing could not reach (after **81**'s billing anomaly and **82**'s live bot at 3,281 passing tests). **The fix:** stage the rows in an unreferenced table, rename the replacement in **empty**, then re-insert by **DML — because `INSERT` decrements the counter and `RENAME` does not**. The header warns, verbatim, ***"DO NOT 'SIMPLIFY' THIS BACK INTO stage-drop-rename. It will pass every test you can run locally and fail in production, which is exactly what happened."*** Verified locally against the real export (byte-identical dumps, empty `foreign_key_check`, 7/7 indexes, no staging tables) with **no remote call from the authoring session**; **REMOTE VERIFICATION the operator's** — 30 bots and 14 proposals unchanged, `foreign_key_check` empty, both `CHECK`s carrying `trailing_stop`, zero staging tables, **21 commands**. **Verified:** `tsc` **14 known errors, none in trailing stop's own logic** (nine `validatedProposalView`, two `derive-prompt`, one `derive.ts`, two pins — all the AI pipeline's missing branches); dashboard `tsc` **clean**; **3298 → 3336, +38 exact, zero regressions**. **22.3 discharged by two files that prove different things** — six `decide()`-level cases establishing the exit is a **level test, not a latched crossing event**, and an end-to-end test through the **real `PriceFeed`** (real `#forwardClosed`, fan-out, watermark, `price_feed_fanout_failed`) proving the candle is genuinely gone and the bot still exits — **mutation-tested**: severing `decide()`'s read of the persisted mark fails it. **⚠ Disclosed limitation:** the end-to-end test delivers prices in-harness, not over the cross-DO RPC hop, because a DO cannot be entered re-entrantly from inside another object's callback — a harness limit, not a code problem. **NOT DONE:** `ASSESS_STRATEGIES` still has **no pin** and the pipeline has no trailing-stop branch, so **bots must be created by hand** (which 22.7 requires anyway); `BotList.tsx`/`BotSummary.tsx` do not render entry price, `highWaterMark` or `trailLevel` (touchpoint 5, cosmetic — the API carries all three); `cycleCount` **stays permanently 0** for this strategy, deliberately, and **must not be read as "no cycle completed" the way it would for DCA**; and **NOT DEPLOYED, NOT RUN** — the migration is applied to testnet but no trailing-stop bot has ever existed, `decide()` has never seen a live candle, and 22.7 gates Stage 2 behind at least a week of live testnet operation first |

OR IS NULL`; deliberately not widened for a 5-minute cron. **Migration 0011** adds partial `idx_alerts_standing_lookup (source, bot_instance_id) WHERE resolved = 0` (0004's precedent); `CREATE INDEX` only, no rebuild, so **[83](83.md)'s deferred-FK-counter hazard does not apply**. Suite **3400 passed / 0 failed** (+27); `tsc` 14 pre-existing errors, unchanged. **The trailing-stop crossing-price fix and retry cap both stay** — they fixed a real design flaw and correctly stopped an unbounded retry, and neither needs reverting. |

Grounds, in descending evidential strength: **depth** (deepest or near-deepest book of any venue tested on every trending/mid-cap coin listed there — ARB, HYPE, UNI — and the only venue of three carrying LIGHTER at all); **reachability** (clean, no network-level block); and **fees** — ~0.16%/0.26% maker/taker vs Coinbase Advanced’s 0.40–0.60%, flagged as **secondary evidence from public sources, NOT verified tonight**, to be re-read from the live fee endpoint of the trading account. `binance.us` recorded as the **stronger venue for BTC/majors specifically** and worth keeping for that narrow case, **not as primary**. **⚠ THE STANDING LIMITATION, CARRIED AS A BUILD REQUIREMENT NOT A CAVEAT:** no venue tested reliably carries the newest, smallest trending coins, so the pipeline must treat **"the AI suggested this coin"** and **"this coin is tradeable with real depth on Kraken"** as **two separate facts**. 21.3 already filters trending candidates to "actually tradable" via `listTradablePairs`, but that answers *listed* — tonight’s data shows *listed* and *tradeable* diverge by up to **127×**, reach **$0 in-band depth**, and admit thin books like LIGHTER; **every monetary value is a decimal STRING** (live) with **none** of the JSON-number exception Gemini’s candles needed; **order state is a real status enum** (`pending`/`open`/`closed`/`canceled`/`expired`) — **closer to Binance’s strings than to Gemini’s `is_live`/`is_cancelled` booleans**, the easiest of the three to map; `instrument` correctly **absent** (spot and futures are separate hosts, as on Binance). Two recorded gaps: **Kraken has no 6-hour candle interval** (240 and 1440 minutes, nothing between), and balances need **`/private/BalanceEx`**, not `/private/Balance`, since only the extended form reports `hold_trade`. **WHAT DOES NOT MAP — four, and none is transcription. (1) PAIR NAMING IS A CATALOGUE, NOT A RULE:** only **44 of 1440** keys differ from their own `altname` (the X/Z prefixing is legacy, confined to ~15 original assets), but **the prefixing is per-FIELD not per-pair** — `ARBUSD` is keyed plainly while its `quote` still reads `ZUSD` — and **every response is a map keyed by a name the client did not choose**, so `result[requestedPair]` is never valid. **A naive `BTC`→`XBT` substitution corrupts real pairs**: `WBTCUSD` and `TBTCUSD` are both live and become the nonexistent `WXBTUSD`/`TXBTUSD` — entry 89’s PART 5 sub-finding (a) reappearing one layer down as a **string-handling** bug. **The only honest approach is a real `AssetPairs` lookup, fetched and cached, not a transformation function**; `toGeminiSymbol`’s one-liner has **no Kraken analogue**, which is why the plan gives Kraken a `catalogue.ts` neither existing venue needed. **(2) THE RATE-LIMIT MODEL IS STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT, NOT NUMERICALLY:** a **continuously-decaying counter**, not a sliding window (so `WeightBudget`’s `#waitFor` computes a wait corresponding to nothing Kraken does, and under-permits); **two independent budgets** — account-wide REST calls (15–20 max, −0.33 to −1/sec by tier) vs a matching-engine ratecount that is **PER PAIR** (60/125/180, −1 to −3.75/sec), which the one-per-account `RateLimiter` DO cannot express; and **cancel cost depends on order AGE, not method name** (**+8** under 5s, decaying to +1 past 300s), which `Record<keyof RestExchangeClient, number>` cannot represent. **⚠ THE WORST CASE IS THE RISK-EXIT PATH:** section 7.2’s halt cancels every open order, a grid bot that just laid a fresh ladder holds exactly the young orders costing the most, and **~7 such cancels exhaust a Starter threshold of 60 on one pair** — precisely the traffic `RISK_EXIT_RESERVE_FRACTION` exists to protect, which it cannot, being denominated in weight-per-account rather than ratecount-per-pair. **No rate-limit headers of any kind** (headers read live, both paths), so `syncFromExchange` has no input and local accounting must be conservative; one partial compensation — `EService:Throttled: <ts>` carries a real timestamp, making Kraken the first venue able to populate `retryAfterMs` from the exchange itself. **This is its own build session and deliberately NOT folded into the base client.** **(3) HTTP 200 ALWAYS:** confirmed live — a bad key returns **200** with `{"error":["EAPI:Invalid key"]}`; **classification must be driven by the error STRING, not the status**, because `classifyStatus`’s whole 4xx/5xx/429 rule set cannot fire when the status is uninformative — and mapping `EService:Unavailable` to `exchange_error` because “the exchange answered” would tell section 5.1 an order was definitely refused when its fate is unknown. **(4) `cancelOrder` CANNOT HONOUR THE CONTRACT STEP 3.1 WROTE FOR IT:** Kraken’s cancel returns **only `{count, pending}`** — no fill data, where Binance echoes the order and Gemini carries `executed_amount` — forcing exactly the follow-up read that comment ruled out as “slower when speed matters most, more rate-limit budget at the worst moment, and racy besides”, now worse because the cancel has just charged up to +8 on the per-pair counter mid-halt. **THE FOUR DECISIONS. (1) TESTNET REFUSES TO BUILD A KRAKEN CLIENT AT ALL**, with a clear reason — Kraken has no sandbox (entry 89), and the alternatives were rejected on principle: pointing both environments at production with “separate low-limit keys” rests safety on **which key was pasted into which secret** instead of on a derivation with no branch capable of producing the wrong host, destroying a guarantee this log has protected twice; and a `FakeExchange` presented as Kraken would be **building the exact failure entry 89 ruled OKX out for** (healthy, fresh, well-formed, describing a market that does not exist) on the venue chosen *because* its data is real. **(2) CANCEL, THEN EXACTLY ONE `QueryOrders` READ, NEVER LOOP** — budget is scarcest during a halt, a bounded read has a cost the gate can reason about beforehand, and an unbounded chase competes for budget with **the remaining cancellations**, so a bot could spend its budget confirming the first cancel and fail to issue the last. **⚠ ACCEPTED TRADEOFF, RECORDED AS DELIBERATE AND NOT A HIDDEN GAP:** the halt path can report a filled quantity **a few moments stale** when `pending: true` and the order fills between cancel and read — a real inaccuracy in a safety path, accepted knowingly because a bounded stale number section 9 already knows how to reconcile beats an unbounded loop that may not finish. Widening the interface to say “cancelled, quantity unknown” was rejected as touching shared code, both clients, `FakeExchange` and every halt call site to express a distinction **one of three venues** produces. **(3) ENFORCE AN 18-CHARACTER CLIENT-ORDER-ID CAP AT GENERATION, FOR KRAKEN ONLY** — Kraken’s `cl_ord_id` is UUID-36 / hex-32 / **free text 18**, and `v1-<slug>-<sequence>` is free text, so `MAX_CLIENT_ORDER_ID_LENGTH = 36` (documented as Binance’s cap) does not apply: `v1-grid-btcusd-01-7` is **20 characters and rejected**. A shorter v2 scheme for all venues was rejected as churn on two working venues for a third’s benefit; **a derived UUID was rejected because it destroys the property the scheme exists for** — `makeClientOrderId`’s own comment says the slug is embedded verbatim so reconciliation can name the owner of an orphaned order, and “a hash would fit any input but would make an orphaned order untraceable”. **⚠ CONDITIONAL:** the cap leaves **slug + digits ≤ 14** while `BOT_INSTANCE_ID_PATTERN` permits 20, so **real `bot_instances` ids must be checked before implementation** — if they do not fit, this decision is not workable as stated. **A second finding recorded because it weakens a stated safety property:** Kraken verifies `cl_ord_id` uniqueness **only across OPEN orders**, so a closed order’s id is reusable — section 5.1’s second layer of duplicate protection is **true on Binance and Gemini and false on Kraken**; the DO attempt records remain the primary layer, the exchange-side backstop simply is not there. **(4) ALWAYS SEND `oflags=fciq`** so the fee asset is **asserted by the request rather than inferred** — Kraken’s fee currency **depends on side** (`fcib`/base when selling, `fciq`/quote when buying) and `QueryTrades` returns a bare `fee` with **no currency field at all**, so “read the fee asset from the fill” has nothing to read. This is the venue where section 5.5’s rule is *literally wrong half the time*; deriving it would rest a safety-relevant value on side → default flag → pair → asset code → un-prefix, any link of which fails silently. **A behaviour change, not a translation** (it changes which asset a **sell** is debited in versus Kraken’s default), which is why it is logged rather than left as an unremarked parameter. **⚠ A FIFTH FINDING, UNRELATED TO KRAKEN AND ALREADY LIVE:** `RateLimitedExchange` defaults `weights` to `BINANCE_METHOD_WEIGHTS` and **neither call site passes one** — confirmed by reading `bot-instance.ts:1221` (`#exchange`, the only path a bot reaches any exchange by) and `reconciliation.ts:364`, both of which pass `priority`/`now`/`label`/`sleep` and **no `weights`**. **So every Gemini account in production is gated against Binance’s cost model today**, despite `exchange-gemini.ts` documenting that Gemini reports a request COUNT and no weight header and deferring the real gate to “the wrapper at dispatch time” — which has been applying Binance’s numbers ever since. Found while tracing how a third venue’s table would be selected; the answer is that **no mechanism to select one exists**. **MUST BE FIXED BEFORE THE KRAKEN RATE-LIMITER WORK BEGINS**, since Kraken’s cost model cannot be wired through a gate that cannot tell which venue it is in front of — and fixing it first keeps it reviewable as a seam correction rather than buried inside a new venue’s integration. **PLANNED STRUCTURE, NOT BUILT:** `src/exchange/kraken/` with `signing.ts` (HMAC-SHA512 over `path + SHA256(nonce + body)`, **base64-DECODED secret**, its own documented example pair since the existing constant holds Binance’s), **`catalogue.ts` (new, no Binance/Gemini analogue)**, `filters.ts`, `parse.ts` (error-array first, no weight-header reader), `client.ts`; plus `workers/exchange-kraken.ts`, a third `case` in `exchange-dispatch.ts` (whose `default`-less `switch` will not compile until wired — the intended forcing function), `ExchangeId` widened, and a migration widening `accounts.exchange`’s CHECK and its partial index. Gating, retrying and entry 89’s liquidity/pair-selection check stay **out** of the client, per the existing division of labour. **BUILD ORDER, and the ordering is load-bearing twice:** (a) fix the weight-table bug standalone — prerequisite for (d); (b) verify real bot-instance-id lengths against the 18-char cap — DECISION 3 is conditional on it, and checking before (c) avoids building a client whose ids the venue rejects; (c) build the base client (signing, catalogue, filters, parsing, all ten methods) without Kraken-specific rate limiting; (d) build the Kraken rate limiter as its own dedicated session, given the structural mismatch above and because it touches a risk control both existing venues run on. **Carried forward, unresolved, not claimed otherwise:** 88.md’s 22.7 observation period still has no honest venue; DECISION 1 does not change that and does not attempt to |
| [91.md](91.md) | 91 | **Kraken, the first three files: a "documented example" that was not documented, a third corruption case the research missed, and 45 real coins that do not fit in `Money`. COMMITTED, NOT DEPLOYED**; `signing.ts` / `catalogue.ts` / `filters.ts` plus tests in three commits (`a43db48`, `b54d975`, `15b1316`), `HEAD` **`15b1316`**, tree clean — 3,694 lines, **127 Kraken tests** (36/45/46), full suite **3,640 passing**, `tsc` clean. **NOTHING IS REACHABLE:** `ExchangeId` is still `"binance" \| "gemini"` (`src/db/schema.ts:34`), so no bot can name Kraken and no resolver builds one — the widening is **deliberately deferred** until `client.ts` exists. ⚠ **THE WORKED EXAMPLE SUPPLIED IN THE PROMPT WAS WRONG** — secret, nonce and expected signature generated from memory, not verified; **the secret did not match Kraken's real published example**, and a test built on it would have gone green while the signer was wrong. Caught **before any code was written around it**, corrected against Kraken's real docs **and** an independent `openssl` reproduction (which also generated three further vectors `V2`–`V4` over a second secret, `V4` reusing `V2`'s nonce and body under a different path to pin that the path is really signed). **THE LESSON, and the most portable thing in the entry: treat any "documented example", hash or exact constant handed over in a prompt as a CLAIM TO VERIFY, not a fact to build on** — a wrong example is more dangerous than a missing one, because a missing one forces a lookup while a wrong one invites a test written around it. Signing's load-bearing detail: Kraken's secret is base64 of a **64-byte** key and must be **decoded**, unlike Binance's and Gemini's `TextEncoder` idiom — the wrong answer that mistake yields is pinned as a **negative** assertion, since "not equal to the documented signature" is too weak to catch a regression. **CATALOGUE FOUND A THIRD CORRUPTION CASE ENTRY 90 DID NOT NAME:** entry 90 rejected a `BTC`→`XBT` substitution citing `WBTCUSD`→`WXBTUSD` and `TBTCUSD`→`TXBTUSD`; live testing against the **full 1440-pair/839-asset** catalogue found **`AIXBTUSD`→`AIBTCUSD`**, broken by the **REVERSE** substitution — invisible to entry 90's search because it reasoned only about the forward direction and looked only for bases *containing* `BTC`, while `AIXBT` *contains* `XBT`. All four non-existence facts verified live. **This is what makes the catalogue-not-transformation approach RIGHT rather than merely cautious:** the research that went looking for counterexamples found two of three, and the third came from running real data through real code. **DECISION — 44 suffixed assets are their own assets:** `XBT.M` (bonded), `DOT.S` (staked) etc. resolve to themselves, never to the base coin, and `normaliseAssetName` **keeps** the `.` that `normalisePairName` strips. **The reason is a wrong number, not naming taste** — a staked/bonded balance is **not freely tradeable**, so aliasing would let the system believe locked funds are available, size an order against them, and manufacture upstream exactly the failure section 9's reconciliation exists to catch. **Recorded as deliberate** precisely because it is also what falls out of doing nothing. ⚠ **DECISION — 45 OF 1440 LIVE PAIRS PUBLISH A `tick_size` FINER THAN `Money`'s `SCALE = 8`** (29 at 9dp, 16 at 10dp), **including `BONKUSD`**, a real actively-traded coin: entry 90's "read `tick_size` directly" and `fromDecimalString`'s strict refusal are each correct and together make 45 tradeable markets unreadable. **DECIDED: coarsen to `1e-8` when the published tick is a power of ten finer than that; `FilterError` when a finer tick is NOT a power of ten.** The coarsening is **a proof, not an approximation** — every multiple of `1e-8` is a multiple of any finer power of ten, so every producible price is one Kraken accepts exactly, and the premise (**all 1440 published ticks are exact powers of ten**) was verified across the whole venue rather than assumed. The non-power-of-ten arm **does not occur today and is handled rather than assumed impossible**. **REJECTED: refuse all 45, mirroring Gemini's simpler `incrementToMoney`** — it would make real tradeable coins permanently inaccessible **for no safety benefit**, since the coarsening is not a guess; *conservatism that buys nothing is a functional regression wearing its clothes*. **`ZERO` was never a candidate** and is recorded to rule out: it means "grid disabled", i.e. unrounded prices through the one check meant to catch them — the **fail-OPEN** direction. Verified: all 1440 parse, **zero omissions**, no `ZERO` tick or step, the 70 pairs on a `1e-8` tick decomposing as 25 genuine + 45 coarsened. ⚠ **THE BUG THE TESTS CAUGHT:** the first power-of-ten check stripped **trailing** zeros but not **leading** ones, so `"0.000000001"` compared as `"000000001" === "1"` — **false** — and **every one of the 45 pairs the rule exists to admit hit the refusal branch**. The rule's effect was **exactly inverted, not degraded**, and inverted *silently*, throwing an articulate and entirely wrong message that read as correct behaviour. **Caught by six real tests failing at once, not by inspection** — which had already passed over the line twice. **LESSON: an off-by-symmetry bug in a numeric string comparison can invert a rule's intended effect entirely** — decimal strings have two ends and they are not interchangeable (trailing zeros insignificant, leading zeros positional); `"0.1"` and `"0.000000001"` are both powers of ten and only the second separates a correct implementation from a broken one, which is the argument for fixtures drawn from the real distribution rather than its easy end. **`pair_decimals` LIES ABOUT THE GRID ON FOUR PAIRS:** it agrees with `tick_size` on **1,436 of 1,440** — which is what makes the shortcut look safe — but `REQUSD`, `CELRUSD`, `VTHOUSD` and `WINUSD` disagree, each in the direction that builds a grid **10× too fine** and gets orders rejected, and on `WINUSD` the `pair_decimals` reading is `1e-9`, **below the money scale**, compounding with the tick collision above. **A test was built against the known-divergent pairs specifically** (asserting the real tick **and** explicitly `not` the `pair_decimals` one, so a field swap fails, plus an order-level `price_off_tick` rejection) — **99.7% agreement between two fields is not evidence they are the same field; it is the reason the difference needs a targeted test**, since a majority-drawn or randomly sampled fixture set would agree with the wrong implementation on every pair it held. **DECISION — `post_only`/`limit_only`/`reduce_only` ALL FAIL CLOSED TO `BREAK`, not `TRADING`** (`online`→`TRADING`, `cancel_only`→`CANCEL_ONLY`, anything else **throws**), **diverging from `gemini/filters.ts`**, which maps its own `post_only` to `TRADING` on the grounds that the ladder places makers: **right about the ladder, wrong about the gate** — one shared `validateOrder` sees only a price and a quantity and **cannot tell whether an order is marketable**, so `TRADING` would let a crossing order past the one check meant to stop it. **THE ACCEPTED COST, recorded as a cost:** three Kraken states sharing `BREAK` means a rejection reads *"is BREAK, not TRADING"* and **cannot say which state caused it** — a real loss in the very log line section 4.3 asks for, accepted because the alternative is widening a shared cross-venue type for one venue's vocabulary. **`limit_only` and `reduce_only` appear on ZERO of 1440 live pairs** (live: `online` 1385, `cancel_only` 38, `post_only` 17) — still mapped **explicitly** rather than left to `default`, with their tests labelled **"CONSTRUCTED, NOT PULLED"**, because a fixture posing as pulled data teaches the wrong shape next time. **DECISION — `ordermin`/`costmin` use `"ceil"`, not the strict parser:** both are **floors**, and a floor rounded **up** is stricter than the venue's (fail-closed), while rounding one **down** sends orders below a minimum Kraken enforces; per `fromDecimalStringRounded`'s own convention the strict parser is for values the system **sends**, not bounds a venue **reports** — the same reasoning that admitted Gemini's 11-decimal balances. Unreachable today (all 1440 publish both within scale) and kept as a direction chosen in advance. **REMAINING, IN ORDER:** `parse.ts` (PROBLEM 3's error-**array** classification read **before** the HTTP status, order/candle/balance parsing, `BalanceEx`'s `free = balance - hold_trade`), `client.ts` (ten methods; DECISION 2's cancel-then-one-read, DECISION 4's `oflags=fciq`), then wiring (workers resolver, exchange-dispatch, **`ExchangeId` widening + migration**, dashboard mirror), then the **rate-limiter redesign as its own later effort** per entry 90 — Kraken's decaying counter and separate matching-engine budget are a redesign, not an adapter. Still outstanding from entry 90: **no 6-hour candle interval** (240 then 1440, nothing between), so no total `Record<CandleInterval, …>` without inventing one; and `Balance` must read **`/private/BalanceEx`** for `hold_trade`. |
| [92.md](92.md) | 92 | **Kraken `parse.ts`: entry 90 was right about the format and wrong about the precision, a safety check that fires falsely on cheap coins, and a bug found by the test nobody thought worth writing. ON DISK, NOT COMMITTED, NOT DEPLOYED**; `parse.ts` (1,456 lines) + `parse.test.ts` (1,462), both untracked, `HEAD` still `2b3308b`, nothing else touched — no shared type, schema, migration, barrel export or spec section. **125 tests**, taking `src/exchange/kraken/` to **252** (36/45/46/125); full suite **3,765 passing**, `tsc` clean; zero regressions established by **stash-and-rerun** (baseline 3,640 — exactly entry 91's figure — with the same single pre-existing file-level error, delta 125). Live: `BONKUSD`'s ticker publishes **12 decimal places**, its candles **9**, `ANKRXBT` **10** — all past `SCALE = 8`, so the strict parser makes **real, actively-traded markets entirely unreadable** (no price, no candles, no backfill). **Read this entry as the accurate version.** Fixed: every Kraken-**reported** value now uses `fromDecimalStringRounded` with a direction **named at the call site**, per `filters.ts`'s precedent — `half-even` for observed prices/quantities (unbiased, and **monotone**, so `vol_exec <= vol` survives rounding and cannot manufacture an overfill), `ceil` for fees. `fromDecimalString` untouched: still the only route for values the system **sends**. **Third appearance of one pattern** (Gemini's 11-dp balance, entry 91's 45 fine `tick_size` pairs, now this): *a venue's published precision is not this system's scale; strict refusal is right for what we send and wrong for what a venue reports.* ⚠ **A SAFETY CHECK WITH A FALSE POSITIVE IT WAS DOCUMENTED AS NOT HAVING:** entry 86's crossed-book detector (spec 5.7 detector 2) claims — correctly, of the check — to have **"no false-positive case to trade against"**, but `gemini/parse.ts` implements it by parsing both sides to `Money` first. `ANKRXBT`'s **live** book is `b 0.0000000601 / a 0.0000000607`: two genuinely different prices that **round to the identical value at 8 decimals**, making `bid >= ask` true and refusing **every price on that pair forever**, plus `BONKUSD` and every other sub-satoshi market. Fixed with `compareDecimalStrings` — exact `BigInt` comparison, no float, **no scale limit** — used only for this check, returning `undefined` (not a guess) for non-decimal input so an absent book reads as *no book to judge*, not a broken one. Real book explicitly asserted **not** to throw, with the reasoning in the test body. **LESSON: a safety check built and verified against one venue's typical price ranges can misfire silently on another venue's real data without either being wrong** — Gemini's version is correct *on Gemini*; the implicit assumption was "prices have at most 8 significant decimals", true of every payload it had ever seen. Caught by running it against Kraken's actual prices rather than assuming it generalised. ⚠ **THE BUG FOUND BY THE THROWAWAY TEST:** an assertion expected to pass trivially (*a pair not in the result should throw*) instead exposed `selectPairResult`'s sole-key fallback **silently returning `BONKUSD`'s candles as `ETHBTC`'s** — plausible data under the wrong name, i.e. entry 89 PART 5's *"silently routing an order into the wrong version of a market"* arriving through the front door, in the module whose entire premise is that Kraken's names are never guessed. Fixed **in `parse.ts`, not `catalogue.ts`**: a sole key that **resolves** must resolve to the pair requested; one the catalogue genuinely cannot place is still accepted, preserving the fallback's purpose. `catalogue.ts` left alone deliberately — it **reports** `matchedBy` so an order path can demand `canonical` while a ticker read accepts `sole-key`, and turning that report into a refusal would take the choice from every caller at once. (`last` must be stripped **before** resolution or the fallback can never fire at all.) **LESSON: the "obviously correct" case is exactly where a boundary condition hides, because nobody writes a targeted test for a case they assume cannot fail** — it survived `catalogue.ts`, its 45 tests, `parse.ts`, and readings of both; same shape as entry 91's leading-zero inversion, **caught by a test failing, not by inspection**. **DECISION — `"6h"` IS `null` IN A TOTAL RECORD, NOT AN OMITTED KEY:** Kraken has no 6-hour OHLC step (1/5/15/30/60/**240**/**1440**/10080/21600 min; live, `interval=360` answers `EGeneral:Invalid arguments`) while `CandleInterval` includes `"6h"`. **No existing convention covers this** — checked: Gemini's and Binance's tables are total because both venues support all seven, and the repo's one `Partial<Record>` (`reconcile.ts:971`) is a **lookup** table, not a **capability** table. **DECIDED: total `Record<CandleInterval, KrakenTimeframe | null>` with `"6h": null`, read through `krakenTimeframe()` which throws.** **Rejected `Partial<Record>`** — it discards the compile-time forcing function; a new interval would leave every venue's table silently incomplete instead of breaking the build, which is the whole reason these unions are closed. **Rejected outright: mapping `"6h"` to 240 or 1440** — answers a six-hour request with four-hour or daily candles and the caller cannot tell; a wrong number wearing the right label. ⚠ **FIXTURE PROVENANCE IS HALF LIVE AND SAYS SO:** public endpoints (AssetPairs/Assets, OHLC, Ticker incl. `ANKRXBT` and `BONKUSD`, Time, and the four error strings — **every one over HTTP 200**) are live-verified 2026-09-03. **Private ones — `AddOrder`, `QueryOrders`, `BalanceEx`, `QueryTrades`, `CancelOrder` — are Kraken's PUBLISHED DOCUMENTATION, not captures**, because **no Kraken credentials exist yet**: every private-endpoint parser is correct against the docs and **unverified against the venue**, so the first credentialled call in `client.ts` is a verification step with expected findings, not a formality. Constructed fixtures (crossed book, negative balance, unknown statuses) labelled at the point of use, per entry 91's rule that *a fixture posing as pulled data teaches the wrong shape next time*. Other confirmed details: `AddOrder`'s `descr.order` **prose is never parsed** (test passes a request contradicting it and asserts the request wins); OHLC **volume is index 6, not 5** — index 5 is **VWAP**, and both other venues put volume at 5, so the cross-venue reading is wrong *and plausible*; `BalanceEx`'s two fields round in **opposite** directions (`balance` floors, `hold_trade` ceils) because the **derived** quantity is `free` where Gemini's is `locked` — with the resulting 2e-8 negative margin permitted **explicitly** and anything beyond it still refused; unrecognised error strings and order statuses both **fail closed**. **REMAINING:** `client.ts` — the last core file and the largest single piece — then wiring (workers resolver, exchange-dispatch, **`ExchangeId` widening + migration**, dashboard mirror, barrel), then the rate-limiter redesign, whose **prerequisite from entry 90 PART 4 (every venue gated by Binance's weight table, `weights` passed by no call site) is still outstanding and still live**. |
